# The components programme — the ledger

_Generated from `ledger.json` by `render-ledger.mjs`. Do not edit this file; edit the JSON and re-render._

This JSON is the single record of the plan; the Markdown beside it is generated from it. Every PR of the programme carries its slot id in its title. A slot is done only when its evidence is written here: the PR number, the prod time and the acceptance test's result line. The agent may change only `status` and `evidence`; a slot is added or its scope changed only through a dated line in `changes` carrying the operator's word, and `scopeHash` (the first 12 hex characters of the SHA-256 of `scope`, rewritten by `node docs/plan/render-ledger.mjs --rehash`) makes such a change a deliberate diff. `pages` lists the routes a done slot serves from rows. The test `lib/design/plan-ledger.test.ts` fails the build when a page is served from rows without a done slot that lists it, when a slot's `scopeHash` does not match its scope, and when the record is malformed. Plan page: artifact 4d804904-63ad-4ddf-8d37-67dcb2d2fcc8.

## Rules the ledger enforces

1. Decision scan before a slot starts: Fixed by · Defaults I take · Needs your word; a non-empty third line stops the slot until the operator answers in one or two sentences with a default. Overnight = decision-free slots only.
2. Gates on every PR: tsc 0 · lint 0 errors · full vitest · cf:build and the dry-run Total Upload quoted (alarm at 80% of 64 MiB) · a browser run on the local server · a review page · a component test from a saved document · parity output for every deleted route file · a fresh-context Sonnet reviewer before merge.
3. No page-specific component; a section that fits none is a question. Sources from the catalogue, never a query. Every component carries a Condition, an authorization scheme, a build option, template options with a preset, and Utilization with History.
4. Every shared object shows where it is used and its history and cannot be deleted while referenced. Text is escaped by default. Exports key files by stable id. Any apply is atomic, validated, staged, refused while locked.
5. Agents: Sonnet or Haiku, one at a time, writing incrementally; building is done directly; fan-outs announced before and accounted after; none above 60% usage.
6. Session ritual: read the rules, the ledger and the git log; name the next slot; start the dev server; smoke-test; one slot at a time; end by writing evidence and the handoff.

R5's browser evidence for the drag was synthetic drag events, which cannot show it. The slot: keep crawlers off the variants at the door, as Google’s faceted-navigation page prefers. | Fixed by: Google Search Central, Managing crawling of faceted navigation URLs: “Use robots.txt to disallow crawling of faceted navigation URLs” as the preferred option when the filtered pages need no indexing, rel=canonical and rel=nofollow as the softer ones; | **R19** The bar under the gesture bar | The bottom bar on an installed Android app (the operator’s Pixel 9, 2026-10-06 ~08:52Z): on the first paint the bar sits under the gesture bar with its labels cut off and settles only after the first scroll. Chrome on Android draws installed apps edge to edge and, on the operator’s Pixel, the live env(safe-area-inset-bottom) arrived only after the first scroll (Chrome’s guide describes it as dynamic); the site never declared viewport-fit=cover. The fix: viewportFit cover on the (app) layout’s viewport export; the bar, the main, the assistant launcher, the nav panel and the wizard read env(safe-area-max-inset-bottom, env(safe-area-inset-bottom, 0px)), the static maximum, so the gesture bar’s height is reserved from the first paint and nothing is positioned by the live inset (which on the Pixel read 0 on the installed app’s first paint and moved in a tab while the viewport’s edge did not); the main’s top padding follows the header’s height (50px + env(safe-area-inset-top)) so the cover opt-in hides nothing under the header on iPhones. The opt-in completed where the browser’s own insetting used to do the work: the header row, the main, the bar and the nav panel pad by env(safe-area-inset-left/right) (a notched iPhone in landscape); the series tab strip and the Data region’s jump strip stick at 3.5rem plus the top inset; the weekend page’s anchors and the race note scroll to 62px/74px plus the top inset; the onboarding wizard’s two bottom paddings read the same max inset. The Data region’s remaining anchors, the calendar’s season view and the post headings’ scroll margins move with the header too; the Roll-of-honour test’s four pins follow the new classes. | Fixed by: the operator’s two screenshots (the bar clipped before the first scroll, right after it); Chrome’s edge-to-edge guide (safe-area-max-inset-* from Chrome 135, the dynamic inset); Next’s Viewport type (viewportFit); the existing env(safe-area-*) padding in AppShell, BottomBar, NavPanel and AssistantWidget · Defaults: the static max inset as the reserve of the bar and of everything that clears it (iOS, with no max inset, falls back to its live inset); no position driven by the live inset; cover on the (app) layout alone, the admin layout’s viewport untouched; the side insets added where cover switches off the browser’s own insetting (the header row, the main, the bar, the nav panel), a default taken so readers see no change in landscape; every header-coupled offset moved with the header (the sticky strips, the weekend anchors, the Data region’s anchors, the season view, the post headings) · **Needs your word:** none beyond the device check: the operator opens the installed app on the Pixel from cold on testing and reads the first paint | On the Pixel 9’s installed app opened from cold (the app swiped away first), pointed at testing: the bar’s labels fully above the gesture bar on the first paint, before any scroll; the home’s first content not under the header; the series tab strip sticks below the header. | **R20** Google’s button in either order | The Sign in with Google button on the sign-in and sign-up pages (one component, components/auth/GoogleButton.tsx) drew only when Google’s script loaded before the site’s nonce: next/script runs its loader once, from the props of its mount, and calls that onLoad when the script lands; the component passed onLoad={draw}, and the mount’s draw held no nonce, so when the nonce landed first the button never drew (the operator’s phone, 2026-10-06). The load becomes a state the draw reads, set through React’s stable setter by both onLoad and onReady and true from the start when Google’s library is already on the page, so the button draws whichever arrives first; a regression test lands the nonce first and fires exactly the handlers the mount kept. | Fixed by: the operator’s screenshot of 2026-10-06 (the sign-in page with the OR divider and an empty space above it); the reproduction on prod at phone width (Google’s script held 2.5 s: the library loaded and no button iframe; the nonce held 2.5 s: the button, 340×44); next/script 16.2.12 (node_modules/next/dist/client/script.js: loadScript runs once from the mount’s props, the load listener calls that onLoad and then onReady, a remount after a load calls onReady, a load already in flight calls onLoad) · Defaults: a state set through React’s stable setter rather than a ref to the latest draw; both handlers given the same setter; the button’s look, size and text unchanged; the nonce and token routes untouched · **Needs your word:** none beyond the merge word | the new case in components/auth/GoogleButton.test.tsx red on the old component and green on the new; the GoogleButton, SignInForm and SignUpForm tests green; npx tsc --noEmit 0; eslint 0; the full suite green; on testing in a browser at phone width, the button drawn (an iframe from accounts.google.com/gsi/button) with Google’s script held 2.5 s and with the nonce held 2.5 s | done · STARTED 2026-10-06 ~17:53Z on “google go”, after the diagnosis of ~13:30Z: the operator’s phone showed no Google button; prod reproduced at phone width (Google’s script held 2.5 s: the library loaded, no button; the nonce held 2.5 s: the button drew); next/script 16.2.12 keeps the onLoad of the mount. ## Phase A — Accounts move to Supabase Auth

Clerk’s free plan signs readers out every seven days; the accounts move to the Supabase project the site already runs, behind a seam of our own, their ids unchanged.

| **O4** One deploy a day, as the foundation | One deploy a day as the foundation: only the operator merges next into main, once a day, and that merge is the only deploy. Claude’s push-guard refuses any pull request not based on next, any merge of a pull request whose base is not next, and merges through the API (.claude/hooks/push-guard.mjs, with its tests). A required check (.github/workflows/main-from-next.yml, job from-next) fails any pull request into main that does not come from this repository’s next, and a ruleset on main requires it, with no force pushes and no deletion. The deploy fills the new build’s cache first, then goes live, then marks the previous build’s cache folder for deletion a day later and records the new build as live (npm run deploy:cf; scripts/keep-live-cache.mts, a marker object, never a bucket listing). CLAUDE.md’s two laws and CONTRIBUTING.md describe the flow. ## Phase F — The performance programme (docs/perf-handoff.md)

The operator’s file of 5 October is the plan: measure, then the front cache, then fewer and cheaper renders, then page weight. Every slot’s acceptance is the file’s gate; the testing Worker before prod; every PR appends its numbers to docs/perf-baselines.md.
